\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.2){\makebox[12.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\cdots$}}\end{picture}\begin{picture}\qbezier{}{0}{0.5}{0.5}{0.5}{1}{0.5}\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.0}}\put(1.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.0}}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{1}{0}{1.0}}\put(0.0,1.0){\line{1}{0}{1.0}}\end{picture}\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.2){\makebox[12.0pt]{$A$}}\end{picture}\end{array}}\end{array}\longrightarrow\begin{array}[]{@{\,}c@{\,}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}}\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.2){\makebox[12.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\cdots$}}\end{picture}\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.2){\makebox[12.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\cdots$}}\end{picture}\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.2){\makebox[12.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\cdots$}}\end{picture}\\
\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.2){\makebox[12.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\cdots$}}\end{picture}\begin{picture}\qbezier{}{0.5}{1}{0.5}{0.8}{0.5}{0.8}\qbezier{}{0}{0.5}{0.2}{0.5}{0.2}{0.5}\qbezier{}{0.5}{0.8}{0.5}{0.5}{0.2}{0.5}\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.0}}\put(1.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.0}}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{1}{0}{1.0}}\put(0.0,1.0){\line{1}{0}{1.0}}\end{picture}\begin{picture}\qbezier{}{0.5}{0}{0.5}{0.2}{0.5}{0.2}\qbezier{}{1}{0.5}{0.8}{0.5}{0.8}{0.5}\qbezier{}{0.8}{0.5}{0.5}{0.5}{0.5}{0.2}\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.0}}\put(1.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.0}}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{1}{0}{1.0}}\put(0.0,1.0){\line{1}{0}{1.0}}\end{picture}\\
\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.2){\makebox[12.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\cdots$}}\end{picture}\begin{picture}\qbezier{}{0}{0.5}{0.5}{0.5}{1}{0.5}\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.0}}\put(1.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.0}}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{1}{0}{1.0}}\put(0.0,1.0){\line{1}{0}{1.0}}\end{picture}\begin{picture}\qbezier{}{0.5}{0}{0.5}{0.3}{0.5}{0.3}\qbezier{}{0.5}{1}{0.5}{0.7}{0.5}{0.7}\qbezier{}{0}{0.5}{0.5}{0.5}{1}{0.5}\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.0}}\put(1.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.0}}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{1}{0}{1.0}}\put(0.0,1.0){\line{1}{0}{1.0}}\end{picture}\end{array}}\end{array}.

Note that this construction gives no additional empty tiles, hence
\mathfrak{s}_{\gamma}(y)=\mathfrak{s}_{s_{t}*\gamma}(y)
.
Since
v_{s_{t}*\gamma}=v_{\gamma}s_{t}<v_{\gamma}
, we have
s_{t}*\gamma>\gamma
and by Proposition
3.1
(4) we have
\left.\Upsilon_{\gamma}(x;y)\right|_{v_{\gamma}s_{t}}=0
.
According to Proposition
3.2
, we have

\left.\Upsilon_{\gamma}(x;y)\right|_{v_{\gamma}}=(y_{v_{\gamma}(t)}-y_{v_{\gamma}(t+1)})\left.\Upsilon_{s_{t}*\gamma}(x;y)\right|_{v_{\gamma}s_{t}}.

By the induction hypothesis, we derive that

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{\gamma}(x;y)|_{v_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=(y_{v_{\gamma}(t)}-y_{v_{\gamma}(t+1)})\Upsilon_{s_{t}\ast\gamma}(x;y)|_{v_{\gamma}s_{t}}

\displaystyle=(y_{v_{\gamma}(t)}-y_{v_{\gamma}(t+1)})\mathfrak{s}_{s_{t}*\gamma}(y)\cdot\prod_{(i,j)\in\overline{\lambda(s_{t}*\gamma)}}(y_{n-j+1}-y_{i})

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{s}_{\gamma}(y)\cdot\prod_{(i,j)\in\overline{\lambda(\gamma)}}(y_{n-j+1}-y_{i}).\qed

4.2.
Proof of Theorem
B

According to
[
29
, Proposition 2.6]
, for any (
p,q
)-clan
\gamma
, the polynomial
\Upsilon_{\gamma}(x;y)
can be expressed as a linear combination of double Schubert polynomials
\mathfrak{S}_{w}(x;y)
with
w\in S_{n}
:

(4.1)

\Upsilon_{\gamma}(x;y)=\sum_{w\in S_{n}}c_{\gamma,w}(y)\mathfrak{S}_{w}(x;y).

Proof of Theorem

B
.

Applying divided difference operator
\partial_{u}
on both sides of equation (
4.1
), we obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{u}\Upsilon_{\gamma}(x;y)=\sum_{w\in S_{n}}c_{\gamma,w}(y)\partial_{u}\mathfrak{S}_{w}(x;y).

Now taking localization at the identity permutation on both sides, combining the fact that
\left.\partial_{u}\mathfrak{S}_{w}(X;Y)\right|_{\mathsf{id}}=1
if
u=w
and 0 otherwise, we obtain

(4.2)

\displaystyle c_{\gamma,w}(y)=\begin{cases}\Upsilon_{w*\gamma}(x;y)|_{\mathsf{id}},&\text{if}\ \ell(w*\gamma)-\ell(\gamma)=\ell(w),\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Now Theorem
B
follows immediately from (
4.2
), Proposition
3.8
and Proposition
3.1
(4).
∎

4.3.
Proof of Theorem
C

Recall that
X_{w}
is the closure of the
B^{-}
-orbit of
V_{\bullet}^{w}=\dot{w}B/B
.
Let
X^{w}
be the closure of the
B
-orbit of
V_{\bullet}^{w}=\dot{w}B/B
.

Proof of Theorem
C
.

Note that we have

\int_{Fl_{n}}[X^{u}]_{T}\smile[X_{v}]_{T}=\delta_{uv}
.
Taking Poincaré pairing with
[X^{w_{0}w}]_{T}
on both sides of

[X_{u_{\gamma}}]_{T}\smile[X_{v}]_{T}=\sum_{w\in S_{n}}d_{u_{\gamma},v}^{w}(y)[X_{w}]_{T},

we obtain

d_{u_{\gamma},v}^{w_{0}w}(y)=\int_{Fl_{n}}[X_{u_{\gamma}}]_{T}\smile[X_{v}]_{T}\smile[X^{w_{0}w}]_{T}.

On the other hand, by
[
27
]
,
Y_{\gamma}
is a Richardson variety if
\gamma
is a non-crossing clan, namely,
[Y_{\gamma}]_{T}=[X_{v_{\gamma}}]_{T}\smile[X^{w_{0}u_{\gamma}}]_{T}
. Expand
[Y_{\gamma}]_{T}
into Schubert classes we have

[X_{v_{\gamma}}]_{T}\smile[X^{w_{0}u_{\gamma}}]_{T}=\sum_{w\in S_{n}}c_{\gamma,w}(y)[X_{w}]_{T},

and

c_{\gamma,w_{0}v}(y)=\int_{Fl_{n}}[X_{v_{\gamma}}]_{T}\smile[X^{w_{0}u_{\gamma}}]_{T}\smile[X^{w_{0}v}]_{T}.

Now reverse the order of
y
and note that
w_{0}Bw_{0}=B^{-}
, we obtain

\displaystyle c_{\gamma,w_{0}v}(\cev{y})

\displaystyle=\int_{Fl_{n}}[w_{0}X_{v_{\gamma}}]_{T}\smile[w_{0}X^{w_{0}u_{\gamma}}]_{T}\smile[w_{0}X^{w_{0}v}]_{T}

\displaystyle=\int_{Fl_{n}}[X^{w_{0}v_{\gamma}}]_{T}\smile[X_{u_{\gamma}}]_{T}\smile[X_{v}]_{T}=d_{u_{\gamma},v}^{w_{0}v_{\gamma}}(y).\qed

5.
Smoothness via localization

It is well known that localization can be used to characterize smoothness. For our purpose, we need the following result, which
appears in the proof of
[
3
, Corollary 19]
. We include a proof here for completeness.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
Y\subseteq G/B
be a closed
T
-equivariant subvariety. Then
Y
is smooth at
u\in W
if and only if
[Y]_{T}|_{u}\in H_{T}^{*}(\mathrm{pt})
satisfies

(5.1)

[Y]_{T}|_{u}=\prod_{(a,b)}(y_{u(a)}-y_{u(b)}),

where the product goes through all
T
-stable curves not in
Y
from
u
with weight
y_{u(a)}-y_{u(b)}
.

Proof.

By
[
3
, Theorem 17]
,
Y
is smooth if and only if the equivariant multiplicity
e_{u}(Y)
of
Y
at
u
is equal to

(5.2)

e_{u}(Y)=\prod_{T^{\prime}}e_{u}(Y^{T^{\prime}})=\prod_{\alpha}\frac{1}{u\alpha},

where
\alpha
are positive roots such that the
T
-stable curves connecting
u
and
us_{\alpha}
is in
Y
.
On the other hand, since the weight of the tangent space at
u
is
\prod_{\alpha>0}u\alpha
, we have

(5.3)

\displaystyle e_{u}(Y)=\frac{[Y]_{T}|_{u}}{\prod_{\alpha>0}u\alpha}.

Combining (
5.3
) and (
5.2
), we are lead to (
5.1
).
∎

To consider the smoothness of the orbit closure
Y_{\gamma}
of any clan
\gamma
at
v_{\gamma}
, by (
5.1
), we need to discuss the
T
-stable curves starting from
v_{\gamma}
not in
Y_{\gamma}
.
Recall that
T
-stable curves on
Fl_{n}
are in bijection with cover relations in the Bruhat order, see
[
6
]
.
Precisely, for
u,w\in S_{n}
with
w=ut_{ab}
for some
a<b
, we can construct a curve

(5.4)

\eta_{u,w}:\mathbb{C}\longrightarrow Fl_{n},\qquad z\longmapsto A(z)B/B,

where the
i
-th column of
A(z)
is
e_{u(i)}+z\delta_{ia}e_{u(b)}
.
The morphism
\eta_{u,w}
can be extended to
\mathbb{P}^{1}
and
\eta_{u,w}(0)=V_{\bullet}^{u}=\dot{u}B/B
and
c(\infty)=V_{\bullet}^{w}=\dot{w}B/B
.
We will refer the curve
\eta_{u,w}
to
the
T
-stable curve connecting
u
and
w
, or

the
T
-stable curve starting from
u
with weight
y_{u(a)}-y_{u(b)}
.

Proposition 5.2
.

Assume
u<w\in S_{n}
with
w=ut_{ab}
for
a<b
(in particular, we have
u(a)<u(b)
).
Let
Y_{\gamma}
be the minimal
K
-orbit closure contains the
T
-stable curve
\eta_{u,w}
.
Then
\gamma
can be constructed as follows. Let
v
be the unique
p
-inverse Grassmannian permutation in the right coset
(S_{p}\times S_{q})u
.

(i)

If
u(b)\leq p
or
p<u(a)
, then
\gamma
is the unique matchless clan with
v_{\gamma}=v
, i.e.,

\text{the $i$-th node of $\gamma$}=\begin{cases}{\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\qbezier{}{0.4}{0.4}{1}{0.4}{1.6}{0.4}\qbezier{}{1}{1}{1}{0.4}{1}{-0.2}\end{picture}{}},&\text{if}\ u(i)\leq p,\\
{\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\qbezier{}{0.5}{0.4}{1}{0.4}{1.5}{0.4}\end{picture}{}},&\text{if}\ u(i)>p.\\
\end{cases}

(ii)

If
u(a)\leq p<u(b)
, then
\gamma
is the unique clan with exactly one
(a,b)
-matching and
v_{\gamma}=v
.

Proof.

Note that we can write the matrix
A(z)
in (
5.4
) as

A(z)=B(z)\dot{u}
, where

B(z)=\mathbf{1}_{n}+zE_{u(b),u(a)},

and
E_{u(b),u(a)}
is the elementary matrix with
1
in the
(u(b),u(a))
position and 0 elsewhere.

In Case (i), we have
B(z)\in GL_{p}\times GL_{q}
.
When
z\neq\infty
,
A(z)B/B
is contained in the orbit

K\dot{u}B/B
.
Since
\gamma
is matchless, we have
\dot{v}_{\gamma}=\dot{v}=\dot{\gamma}
. Thus the orbit
Y_{\gamma}^{\circ}
contains
\dot{v}B/B
, or equivalently,
\dot{u}B/B
. Therefore, the orbit closure
Y_{\gamma}
contains
A(z)
, i.e., the
T
-stable curve connecting
u
and
w
.

Let us consider Case (ii).
Let
y\in S_{p}\times S_{q}
be such that
yu=v
.
Since
\dot{y}\in GL_{p}\times GL_{q}
, the curve
\eta_{u,w}
is contained in
Y_{\gamma}^{\circ}
if and only if the curve
\eta_{yu,yw}
is contained in
Y_{\gamma}^{\circ}
. Therefore, we can assume that
u=v
. Since
\gamma
has only one
(a,b)
-matching with
v_{\gamma}=v
, we have
\dot{\gamma}=\dot{v}_{\gamma}+E_{u(b),a}=\dot{v}+E_{u(b),a}
.
When
z\neq 0
, taking the
\{u(a),u(b)\}\times\{a,b\}
minor, by the identity

\left[\begin{matrix}1&0\\
z&1\end{matrix}\right]=\left[\begin{matrix}1&0\\
0&z\end{matrix}\right]\left[\begin{matrix}1&0\\
1&1\end{matrix}\right]\left[\begin{matrix}1&0\\
0&z^{-1}\end{matrix}\right],

we find

A(z)B/B\in K\dot{\gamma}B/B=Y_{\gamma}^{\circ}.

This proves Case (ii).

Since
A(z)B/B\in K\dot{\gamma}B/B=Y_{\gamma}^{\circ}
for any
z\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
in both cases, we find that any
Y_{\tau}
containing the curve
\eta_{u,w}
satisfies
Y_{\gamma}^{\circ}\subseteq Y_{\tau}
, so
Y_{\gamma}\subseteq Y_{\tau}
. That is,
Y_{\gamma}
is the minimal.
∎

Theorem 5.3
.

Let
\gamma
be a (
p,q
)-clan.
Then the
T
-stable curve starting from
v=v_{\gamma}
with weight
y_{v(a)}-y_{v(b)}
(
a<b
) is contained in
Y_{\gamma}
if and only if either one of the following holds:

(1)

\max\{v(a),v(b)\}\leq p
or
\min\{v(a),v(b)\}>p
.

(2)

v(a)\leq p<v(b)
with
i\leq a<b\leq j
for some
(i,j)
-matching in
\gamma
.

To prove Theorem
5.3
, we need to recall the partial order on
K
-orbit closures under containment which can be characterized by the Bruhat order on clans.

Proposition 5.4

(
[
28
, Theorem 1.2]
)
.

Let
\gamma,\tau
be two
(p,q)
-clans with
p+q=n
.
Then
Y_{\gamma}\subseteq Y_{\tau}
if and only if the following hold for all
i<j
:

\gamma(i;{\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\qbezier{}{0.4}{0.4}{1}{0.4}{1.6}{0.4}\qbezier{}{1}{1}{1}{0.4}{1}{-0.2}\end{picture}{}})\geq\tau(i;{\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\qbezier{}{0.4}{0.4}{1}{0.4}{1.6}{0.4}\qbezier{}{1}{1}{1}{0.4}{1}{-0.2}\end{picture}{}}),\qquad\gamma(i;{\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\qbezier{}{0.5}{0.4}{1}{0.4}{1.5}{0.4}\end{picture}{}})\geq\tau(i;{\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\qbezier{}{0.5}{0.4}{1}{0.4}{1.5}{0.4}\end{picture}{}}),\qquad\gamma(i;j)\leq\tau(i;j),

where

\displaystyle\gamma(i;{\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\qbezier{}{0.4}{0.4}{1}{0.4}{1.6}{0.4}\qbezier{}{1}{1}{1}{0.4}{1}{-0.2}\end{picture}{}})

\displaystyle=\texttt{\#}\{{\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\qbezier{}{0.4}{0.4}{1}{0.4}{1.6}{0.4}\qbezier{}{1}{1}{1}{0.4}{1}{-0.2}\end{picture}{}}\text{ and matchings in the first $i$ nodes of }\gamma\},

\displaystyle\gamma(i;{\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\qbezier{}{0.5}{0.4}{1}{0.4}{1.5}{0.4}\end{picture}{}})

\displaystyle=\texttt{\#}\{{\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\qbezier{}{0.5}{0.4}{1}{0.4}{1.5}{0.4}\end{picture}{}}\text{ and matchings in the first $i$ nodes of }\gamma\},

\displaystyle\gamma(i;j)

\displaystyle=\texttt{\#}\{(s,t)\text{-matching of }\gamma\text{ with }s\leq i<j\leq t\}.

The partial order under containment on orbit closures defines a partial order, called the strong Bruhat order, on clans. That is, we can define
\gamma\preceq\tau
in the strong Bruhat order if and only if
Y_{\gamma}\subseteq Y_{\tau}
.

Lemma 5.5
.

In Proposition
5.4
, the condition

\gamma(i;{\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\qbezier{}{0.4}{0.4}{1}{0.4}{1.6}{0.4}\qbezier{}{1}{1}{1}{0.4}{1}{-0.2}\end{picture}{}})\geq\tau(i;{\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\qbezier{}{0.4}{0.4}{1}{0.4}{1.6}{0.4}\qbezier{}{1}{1}{1}{0.4}{1}{-0.2}\end{picture}{}})
for all
i\in[n]:=\{1,2,\ldots,n\}
is equivalent to
u_{\gamma}\geq u_{\tau}
under Bruhat order.
Similarly,

\gamma(i;{\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\qbezier{}{0.5}{0.4}{1}{0.4}{1.5}{0.4}\end{picture}{}})\geq\tau(i;{\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\qbezier{}{0.5}{0.4}{1}{0.4}{1.5}{0.4}\end{picture}{}})
for all
i\in[n]

is equivalent to
v_{\gamma}\geq v_{\tau}
.

Proof.

Notice that
\gamma(i;{\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\qbezier{}{0.4}{0.4}{1}{0.4}{1.6}{0.4}\qbezier{}{1}{1}{1}{0.4}{1}{-0.2}\end{picture}{}})
is also equal to the number of
and right endpoints in the first
i
nodes of
\gamma
.
Let
\gamma^{\prime}
(resp.,
\tau^{\prime}
) be the matchless clan obtained from
\gamma
(resp.,
\tau
) by replacing each left endpoint with
and right endpoint with
. Then we have
\gamma^{\prime}(i;{\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\qbezier{}{0.4}{0.4}{1}{0.4}{1.6}{0.4}\qbezier{}{1}{1}{1}{0.4}{1}{-0.2}\end{picture}{}})=\gamma(i;{\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\qbezier{}{0.4}{0.4}{1}{0.4}{1.6}{0.4}\qbezier{}{1}{1}{1}{0.4}{1}{-0.2}\end{picture}{}})
and
u_{\gamma}=u_{\gamma^{\prime}}
. Therefore,

\gamma(i;{\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\qbezier{}{0.4}{0.4}{1}{0.4}{1.6}{0.4}\qbezier{}{1}{1}{1}{0.4}{1}{-0.2}\end{picture}{}})\geq\tau(i;{\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\qbezier{}{0.4}{0.4}{1}{0.4}{1.6}{0.4}\qbezier{}{1}{1}{1}{0.4}{1}{-0.2}\end{picture}{}})\Leftrightarrow\gamma^{\prime}(i;{\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\qbezier{}{0.4}{0.4}{1}{0.4}{1.6}{0.4}\qbezier{}{1}{1}{1}{0.4}{1}{-0.2}\end{picture}{}})\geq\tau^{\prime}(i;{\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\qbezier{}{0.4}{0.4}{1}{0.4}{1.6}{0.4}\qbezier{}{1}{1}{1}{0.4}{1}{-0.2}\end{picture}{}})\Leftrightarrow\gamma^{\prime}(i;{\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\qbezier{}{0.5}{0.4}{1}{0.4}{1.5}{0.4}\end{picture}{}})\leq\tau^{\prime}(i;{\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\qbezier{}{0.5}{0.4}{1}{0.4}{1.5}{0.4}\end{picture}{}}),\text{for\ }i\in[n].

For any two
q
-inverse Grassmannian permutations
w_{1},w_{2}
, we have
w_{1}\leq w_{2}
under Bruhat order
\Leftrightarrow

w_{1}^{-1}(i)\leq w_{2}^{-1}(i)
for
i\in[q]

\Leftrightarrow

\texttt{\#}(w_{1}[i]\cap[q])\geq\texttt{\#}(w_{2}[i]\cap[q])
for
i\in[n]
. Thus

\gamma^{\prime}(i;{\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\qbezier{}{0.5}{0.4}{1}{0.4}{1.5}{0.4}\end{picture}{}})\leq\tau^{\prime}(i;{\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\qbezier{}{0.5}{0.4}{1}{0.4}{1.5}{0.4}\end{picture}{}})\Leftrightarrow\texttt{\#}(u_{\gamma^{\prime}}[i]\cap[q])\leq\texttt{\#}(u_{\tau^{\prime}}[i]\cap[q])\Leftrightarrow u_{\gamma^{\prime}}\geq u_{\tau^{\prime}}.

Similarly,
\gamma^{\prime}(i;{\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\qbezier{}{0.5}{0.4}{1}{0.4}{1.5}{0.4}\end{picture}{}})\geq\tau^{\prime}(i;{\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\qbezier{}{0.5}{0.4}{1}{0.4}{1.5}{0.4}\end{picture}{}})
is equivalent to
\tau^{\prime}
has as least as many
’s than
\gamma^{\prime}
among the first
i
positions, that is,
v_{\gamma^{\prime}}\geq v_{\tau^{\prime}}
.
∎

Corollary 5.6
.

Let
\gamma
be a (
p,q
)-clan.
If the torus fixed point
V^{w}_{\bullet}=\dot{w}B/B\in Y_{\gamma}
, then

v_{\gamma}\leq w
.

Proof.

Let
\tau
be the matchless clan such that
v_{\tau}
is the minimal element in
(S_{p}\times S_{q})w
.
Then
v_{\tau}\leq w
and
\dot{v}_{\tau}B/B\in Y_{\tau}^{\circ}
.
Since
w=yv_{\tau}
for some
y\in S_{p}\times S_{q}
, we have
\dot{w}B/B\in Y^{\circ}_{\tau}
.
Since
\dot{w}B/B\in Y_{\gamma}
, we have
Y_{\tau}\subseteq Y_{\gamma}
.
By Proposition
5.4
, we have
\tau(i;{\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\qbezier{}{0.5}{0.4}{1}{0.4}{1.5}{0.4}\end{picture}{}})\geq\gamma(i;{\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\qbezier{}{0.5}{0.4}{1}{0.4}{1.5}{0.4}\end{picture}{}})
for
i\in[n]
and by Lemma
5.5
, we have
w\geq v_{\tau}\geq v_{\gamma}
.
∎

We need two more corollaries which are immediate from
[
28
, Theorem 2.8]
.

Corollary 5.7
.

Let
\gamma
be a
(p,q)
-clan with a
at the
i
-th node and a
at the
j
-th node,
i<j
. Let
\gamma^{\prime}
be the clan obtained from
\gamma
by matching the
i
-th and
j
-th node. Then
Y_{\gamma}\subseteq Y_{\gamma^{\prime}}
.

\gamma={\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\put(0.5,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\put(1.0,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\put(1.5,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\end{picture}\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\qbezier{}{0.4}{0.4}{1}{0.4}{1.6}{0.4}\qbezier{}{1}{1}{1}{0.4}{1}{-0.2}\end{picture}\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\put(0.5,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\put(1.0,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\put(1.5,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\end{picture}\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\put(0.5,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\put(1.0,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\put(1.5,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\end{picture}\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\qbezier{}{0.5}{0.4}{1}{0.4}{1.5}{0.4}\end{picture}\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\put(0.5,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\put(1.0,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\put(1.5,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\end{picture}{}}\qquad\gamma^{\prime}={\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\put(0.5,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\put(1.0,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\put(1.5,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\end{picture}\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\qbezier{}{1}{0.4}{4}{4}{7}{0.4}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\qbezier{}{1}{0.4}{4}{4}{7}{0.4}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\put(1.0,0.4){\circle*{0.7}}\end{picture}\rule{0.0pt}{17.99995pt}\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\put(0.5,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\put(1.0,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\put(1.5,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\end{picture}\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\put(0.5,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\put(1.0,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\put(1.5,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\end{picture}\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\put(1.0,0.4){\circle*{0.7}}\end{picture}\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\put(0.5,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\put(1.0,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\put(1.5,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\end{picture}{}}

Corollary 5.8
.

Let
\gamma
and
\tau
be two clans with

v_{\gamma}=v_{\tau}
.
If
\gamma
has a unique
(i_{1},j_{1})
-matching,
\tau
has a unique
(i_{2},j_{2})
-matching, and
i_{1}\leq i_{2}\leq j_{2}\leq j_{1}
, then
Y_{\tau}\subseteq Y_{\gamma}
.

\begin{matrix}\tau={\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\put(0.5,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\put(1.0,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\put(1.5,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\end{picture}\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\qbezier{}{0.4}{0.4}{1}{0.4}{1.6}{0.4}\qbezier{}{1}{1}{1}{0.4}{1}{-0.2}\end{picture}\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\put(0.5,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\put(1.0,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\put(1.5,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\end{picture}\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\qbezier{}{1}{0.4}{3}{3}{5}{0.4}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\qbezier{}{1}{0.4}{3}{3}{5}{0.4}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\put(1.0,0.4){\circle*{0.7}}\end{picture}\rule{0.0pt}{14.39996pt}\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\put(0.5,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\put(1.0,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\put(1.5,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\end{picture}\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\put(1.0,0.4){\circle*{0.7}}\end{picture}\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\put(0.5,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\put(1.0,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\put(1.5,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\end{picture}\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\qbezier{}{0.5}{0.4}{1}{0.4}{1.5}{0.4}\end{picture}\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\put(0.5,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\put(1.0,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\put(1.5,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\end{picture}{}}\qquad\gamma={\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\put(0.5,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\put(1.0,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\put(1.5,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\end{picture}\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\qbezier{}{1}{0.4}{7}{7}{13}{0.4}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\qbezier{}{1}{0.4}{7}{7}{13}{0.4}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\put(1.0,0.4){\circle*{0.7}}\end{picture}\rule{0.0pt}{28.79993pt}\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\put(0.5,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\put(1.0,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\put(1.5,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\end{picture}\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\qbezier{}{0.4}{0.4}{1}{0.4}{1.6}{0.4}\qbezier{}{1}{1}{1}{0.4}{1}{-0.2}\end{picture}\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\put(0.5,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\put(1.0,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\put(1.5,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\end{picture}\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\qbezier{}{0.5}{0.4}{1}{0.4}{1.5}{0.4}\end{picture}\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\put(0.5,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\put(1.0,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\put(1.5,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\end{picture}\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\put(1.0,0.4){\circle*{0.7}}\end{picture}\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\put(0.5,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\put(1.0,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\put(1.5,0.4){\circle*{0.3}}\end{picture}{}}\\
\end{matrix}

Now we are ready to prove Theorem
5.3
.

Proof of Theorem
5.3
.

We first show that the curves satisfying conditions in Case (1) or Case (2) must lie in
Y_{\gamma}
.
By Proposition
5.2
(i), the curves in Case (1) are contained in
Y_{\tau}
, where
\tau
is the matchless clan with
v_{\tau}=v_{\gamma}
.
By Corollary
5.7
, the closed orbit
Y_{\tau}
is contained in
Y_{\gamma}
.
So the curves in Case (1) are contained in
Y_{\gamma}
.

By Proposition
5.2
(ii), the curves in Case (2) are contained in some
Y_{\tau}
, such that
\tau
has only one
(a,b)
-matching with
v_{\tau}=v_{\gamma}
.
By Corollary
5.8
, if
\gamma^{\prime}
has only one
(i,j)
-matching and
v_{\gamma^{\prime}}=v_{\tau}
, then

Y_{\tau}\subseteq Y_{\gamma^{\prime}}
.
By Corollary
5.7
, if
\gamma
has an
(i,j)
-matching, then
Y_{\gamma^{\prime}}\subseteq Y_{\gamma}
.
Thus the curves in Case (2) are contained in
Y_{\gamma}
.

Conversely, assume the
T
-stable curve
\eta_{v,w}
from
v=v_{\gamma}
to
w=vt_{ab}
is contained in
Y_{\gamma}
.
If
v(b)\leq p<v(a)
, then
w=vt_{ab}<v
.
By Corollary
5.6
,
\dot{w}B/B\notin Y_{\gamma}
, so the curve
\eta_{v,w}
cannot be contained in
Y_{\gamma}
.
Thus we can assume
v(a)\leq p<v(b)
. By Proposition
5.2
(ii), there is a clan
\tau
with only one
(a,b)
-matching such that
Y_{\tau}
is the minimal orbit closure which contains the curve
\eta_{v,w}
. Thus
Y_{\tau}\subseteq Y_{\gamma}
.
By Proposition
5.4
,

1=\tau(a,b)\leq\gamma(a,b)
.
In particular, there must be some
(i,j)
-matching in
\gamma
with
i\leq a<b\leq j
.
∎

Finally, we are in a position to prove Theorem
D
.

Proof of Theorem
D
.

(1)
\Leftrightarrow
(2).
By Theorem
5.3
,
the
T
-stable curve starting from
v=v_{\gamma}
with weight
y_{v(a)}-y_{v(b)}
(
a<b
) is NOT contained in
Y_{\gamma}
if and only if either one of the following two cases holds:

(I)

v(b)\leq p<v(a)
or

(II)

v(a)\leq p<v(b)
but no
(i,j)
-matching in
\gamma
such that

i\leq a<b\leq j
.

Recall that
v_{\gamma}
is obtained by assigning each
or left endpoint with
1,2,\ldots,p
from left to right, and assigning
or right endpoint with
p+1,p+2,\ldots,n
from left to right.
Note that if the
i
-th node of
\gamma
is
or left endpoint (resp.,
or right endpoint), then the
i
-th step on the southeast boundary of
\lambda(\gamma)
lies in the
v_{\gamma}(i)
-th row (resp.,
(n+1-v_{\gamma}(i))
-th column).

Case (I) implies that the
a
-th node of
\gamma
is a
or a right endpoint and the
b
-th node is a
or left endpoint. Since
a<b
, the tile at
(v(b),n+1-v(a))
is in
\overline{\lambda(\gamma)}
. See the
*
tiles in (
5.5
) as an example. Therefore, we have

\prod_{(a,b)\text{ in Case (I)}}(y_{v(a)}-y_{v(b)})=\prod_{(i,j)\in\overline{\lambda(\gamma)}}(-y_{i}+y_{n-j+1}).

For Case (II), the
a
-th node of
\gamma
is a
or a left endpoint and the
b
-th node is a
or right endpoint. Since
a<b
, the tile at
(v(b),n+1-v(a))
belongs to
\lambda(\gamma)
.
Because no
(i,j)
-matching exists for
i\leq a<b\leq j
, there are no pipes northwest to the box at
(v(a),n+1-v(b))\in\lambda(\gamma)
. Thus the product of weights in Case (II) corresponds to

\prod_{(a,b)\text{ in Case (II)}}(y_{v(a)}-y_{v(b)})=\prod_{(i,j)}(y_{i}-y_{n+1-j}),

where the product runs over empty tiles
(i,j)\in\lambda(\gamma)
such that there are no pipes northwest of
(i,j)
in the Rothe BPD
D(\gamma)
.
See the shadowed tiles in (
5.5
) for an example.

By Lemma
5.1
,
Y_{\gamma}
is smooth at
v_{\gamma}
if and only if

[Y_{\gamma}]_{T}|_{v_{\gamma}}=\prod_{(a,b)\ \text{in Case (I) or (II)}}(y_{v(a)}-y_{v(b)}).

The equivalence of (1) and (2) follows from
Theorem
A

and the definition of
\mathfrak{s}_{\gamma}(x)
.

(2)
\Leftrightarrow
(3).
Note that if
\tau
appears as a pattern of
\gamma
, then
D(\tau)
can be obtained from
D(\gamma)
by deleting some rows and columns.
So if
D(\gamma)
contains any of the 5 patterns in Theorem
D
, then there exists an empty tile southeast to a pipe, thus we can obtain another BPD fragment by applying a droop operation.
This implies (2)
\Rightarrow
(3).

Conversely, we show that if
\gamma
has more than one BPD fragment, then
\gamma
must contains one of the 5 patterns. By Proposition
2.1
, any BPD fragment can be obtained from
D(\gamma)
by applying droop operations. Thus there exists some pipe with an empty tile
\begin{array}[]{@{\,}c@{\,}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}}\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.0}}\put(1.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.0}}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{1}{0}{1.0}}\put(0.0,1.0){\line{1}{0}{1.0}}\end{picture}\end{array}}\end{array}
southeast to this pipe in
D(\gamma)
. Suppose that such a
\begin{array}[]{@{\,}c@{\,}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}}\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.0}}\put(1.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.0}}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{1}{0}{1.0}}\put(0.0,1.0){\line{1}{0}{1.0}}\end{picture}\end{array}}\end{array}
is at position

(v_{\gamma}(a),n+1-v_{\gamma}(b))
. Then the
a
-th node of
\gamma
must be a
or the left endpoint of some
(a,j)
-matching with
j<b

(since if
j\geq b
, then the box at
(v_{\gamma}(a),n+1-v_{\gamma}(b))
will be
\begin{array}[]{@{\,}c@{\,}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}}\begin{picture}\qbezier{}{0}{0.5}{0.5}{0.5}{1}{0.5}\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.0}}\put(1.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.0}}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{1}{0}{1.0}}\put(0.0,1.0){\line{1}{0}{1.0}}\end{picture}\end{array}}\end{array}
or
\begin{array}[]{@{\,}c@{\,}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}}\begin{picture}\qbezier{}{0.5}{0}{0.5}{0.2}{0.5}{0.2}\qbezier{}{1}{0.5}{0.8}{0.5}{0.8}{0.5}\qbezier{}{0.8}{0.5}{0.5}{0.5}{0.5}{0.2}\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.0}}\put(1.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.0}}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{1}{0}{1.0}}\put(0.0,1.0){\line{1}{0}{1.0}}\end{picture}\end{array}}\end{array}
). Similarly, the
b
-th node of
\gamma
must be a
or the right endpoint of some
(i,b)
-matching with
i>a

This completes the proof of (3)
\Rightarrow
(2).
∎

Example 5.9
.

Here the shadowed tiles correspond to Case (II), and the
*
marks the Case (I).

Finally, we remark that, unlike the case of Richardson varieties, the fact that the
K
-orbit closure
Y_{\gamma}
is smooth at
v_{\gamma}
and
w_{0}u_{\gamma}
does not guarantee that
Y_{\gamma}
is smooth.

Example 5.10
.

Let
\gamma={\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\qbezier{}{1}{0.4}{3}{3}{5}{0.4}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\qbezier{}{1}{0.4}{3}{3}{5}{0.4}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\put(1.0,0.4){\circle*{0.7}}\end{picture}\rule{0.0pt}{14.39996pt}\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\qbezier{}{1}{0.4}{3}{3}{5}{0.4}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\qbezier{}{1}{0.4}{3}{3}{5}{0.4}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\put(1.0,0.4){\circle*{0.7}}\end{picture}\rule{0.0pt}{14.39996pt}\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\put(1.0,0.4){\circle*{0.7}}\end{picture}\begin{picture}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\put(1.0,0.4){\circle*{0.7}}\end{picture}{}}
.
The orbit closure admits the following explicit description

Y_{\gamma}=\{V_{\bullet}\in Fl_{4}\mid XV_{1}\subset V_{3}\}

where
X=\operatorname{diag}(1,1,0,0)
; see
[
5
, Corollary 3.7]
.
Note that
Y_{\gamma}
is a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle over

Z=\{V_{1}\subset V_{3}\mid XV_{1}\subset V_{3}\}
.
By Plücker embedding,
Z
can be identified
as the subvariety of

\mathbb{P}^{3}\times\mathbb{P}^{3}

cut out by the equations

\begin{cases}x_{1}y_{1}+x_{2}y_{2}=0,\\
x_{3}y_{3}+x_{4}y_{4}=0,\end{cases}\qquad\text{where }\begin{array}[]{r@{\,}l}{}[x_{1}:x_{2}:x_{3}:x_{4}]&\in\mathbb{P}^{3},\\
{}[y_{1}:y_{2}:y_{3}:y_{4}]&\in\mathbb{P}^{3}.\end{array}

The singular loci can be described as

\{x_{1}=x_{2}=y_{1}=y_{2}=0\}\cup\{x_{3}=x_{4}=y_{3}=y_{4}=0\}.

Using this, it is easy to check
Y_{\gamma}
is nonsingular at
\dot{w}B/B
for
w\in S_{4}
with

w(1)\notin\{1,2\}\ni w(4)\quad\text{or}\quad w(4)\notin\{1,2\}\ni w(1).

In particular,
Y_{\gamma}
is smooth at
v_{\gamma}=\mathsf{id}
.
Similarly,
Y_{\gamma}
is smooth at
w_{0}u_{\gamma}
.
However,
Y_{\gamma}
itself is not smooth.

2,2
)-clans
